through on instructions and fails to finish schoolwork, chores, or duties in the workplace.\n  manifestation: If someone gives me a list of things to follow up on after a site walk, I’ll handle the first one or two, then get pulled into something else and later find out I never sent the email, called the subcontractor, or closed out the issue.\n- key: S005\n  name: Organization_Difficulty\n  description: Often has difficulty organizing tasks and activities.\n  manifestation: My truck, desk, and phone notes are all a mess, and when I’ve got RFIs, inspections, budget stuff, and crew problems stacked up, I don’t know what to hit first so I jump around and everything runs late.\n- key: S007\n  name: Losing_Items\n  description: Often loses things necessary for tasks or activities.\n  manifestation: I’m always patting my pockets or digging through the truck for my keys, tape, notebook, marked-up drawings, or even the phone I just had in my hand a minute ago.\n- key: S011\n  name: Leaving_Seat\n  description: Often leaves seat in situations when remaining seated is expected.\n  manifestation: In meetings where everybody else can sit through the whole thing, I’m the guy standing up, walking to the coffee, stepping outside to check something, or making an excuse to move around.\n- key: S015\n  name: Excessive_Talking\n  description: Often talks excessively.\n  manifestation: Once I get going about a problem on the job, I keep talking and explaining from every angle, even when I can tell the other guys are trying to move on.\n- key: S016\n  name: answer too fast and sometimes realize I missed the important part they hadn’t said yet.\n- key: S017\n  name: Waiting_Turn_Difficulty\n  description: Often has difficulty waiting his or her turn.\n  manifestation: Waiting for my turn on a call, at the permit office, or in a line drives me nuts, and I’m checking the time, shifting around, and fighting the urge to push ahead.\n- key: S018\n  name: Interrupting_Intruding\n  description: Often interrupts or intrudes on others (e.g., butts into conversations, games, or activities).\n  manifestation: I’ll walk up on two subcontractors talking or someone working through paperwork and jump right in with my own point before I’ve been asked, and it rubs people the wrong way.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: Onset prior to age 12 years\n  manifestation: Back in elementary school, teachers were already telling my parents I couldn’t stay in my seat, talked out of turn, lost homework, and rushed through directions before I really understood them.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: At work it has gotten bad enough that missed What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I’m having a hard time keeping my attention on things I need to. I don’t mean it badly, but it definitely rubs people the wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and activities, or completing them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. My truck’s a mess, my desk is a mess, and even the notes in my phone are all over the place. When I’ve got RFIs, inspections, budget stuff, and crew issues all coming at me, I have a hard time figuring out what to do first. I end up jumping from one thing to another, and then stuff runs late.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're physically restless, like you have excess energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’m on my feet a lot for work anyway, so it’s hard to tell what’s normal. I don’t know that I’d call it excess energy exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with attention and impulsivity, or is this something that started more recently?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on since I was a kid. I remember back in elementary school, teachers were already telling my parents I couldn’t stay in my seat, I’d talk out of turn, lose homework, and rush through directions before I really understood what I was supposed to do. So no, it’s not really new.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have trouble engaging in quiet leisure activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t really do a lot of quiet leisure stuff, so it’s hard for me to say. If I’m sitting around, I usually end up finding something to do, but I don’t know if that’s restlessness or just habit.\n\nDoctor: Do you have problems with anxiety or depression?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say that’s been the main issue for me. I get stressed like anyone else, but I haven’t really thought of it as anxiety or depression.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're always on the go, or like you can't slow down?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I do keep busy most of the time, especially with work, but I don’t know if I’d say I “can’t slow down.” I guess I haven’t paid close attention to it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not at work, like when you're watching TV or sitting with family?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, I haven’t really paid attention to that. If I’m watching TV I might shift around or get up, but I’m not sure if it’s more than normal. My family hasn’t really said anything about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you need to be active or moving when you're not working?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, outside of work I don’t really think about whether I’m needing to move. I might get up and do stuff around the house, but I couldn’t say if that’s because I need to be active or just because there’s always something to do.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had problems at work or in your relationships because of your difficulties with attention or impulsivity?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has caused problems. At work, I’ve missed follow-ups or didn’t keep site coordination organized enough, and it got to the point my employer brought it up formally. At home, my wife gets pretty worn out having to remind me about things I said I’d take care of. It’s frustrating, honestly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I’m having a hard time keeping my attention on things I need to. Like, I’ll start a safety briefing or a plan review really trying to focus, and within a few minutes my mind’s already jumped to three other problems on the job. Then I miss stuff I know I should’ve caught.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety, like you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I do move around a lot because of the job, but as far as feeling like I can’t sit still… I haven’t really paid attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others or blurt out answers before they finish speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I’ll cut people off because I think I know where they’re going with the question, and I answer too quick. Then sometimes I realize I missed the important part they hadn’t even said yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others or acting on impulse in non-work situations, like in conversations with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Even outside work, I’ll step into a conversation or jump in while someone’s in the middle of sorting something out, like paperwork or whatever, and I’ll throw my point in before anyone asks. I don’t mean it badly, but it definitely rubs people the wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and activities, or completing them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. My truck’s a mess, my desk is a mess, and even the notes in my phone are all over the place. When I’ve got RFIs, inspections, budget stuff, and crew issues all coming at me, I have a hard time figuring out what to do first. I end up jumping from one thing to another, and then stuff runs late.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're physically restless, like you have excess energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’m on my feet a lot for work anyway, so it’s hard to tell what’s normal. I don’t know that I’d call it excess energy exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with attention and impulsivity, or is this something that started more recently?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on since I was a kid. I remember back in elementary school, teachers were already telling my parents I couldn’t stay in my seat, I’d talk out of turn, lose homework, and rush through directions before I really understood what I was supposed to do. So no, it’s not really new.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have trouble engaging in quiet leisure activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t really do a lot of quiet leisure stuff, so it’s hard for me to say. If I’m sitting around, I usually end up finding something to do, but I don’t know if that’s restlessness or just habit.\n\nDoctor: Do you have problems with anxiety or depression?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say that’s been the main issue for me. I get stressed like anyone else, but I haven’t really thought of it as anxiety or depression.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're always on the go, or like you can't slow down?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I do keep busy most of the time, especially with work, but I don’t know if I’d say I “can’t slow down.” I guess I haven’t paid close attention to it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not at work, like when you're watching TV or sitting with family?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, I haven’t really paid attention to that. If I’m watching TV I might shift around or get up, but I’m not sure if it’s more than normal. My family hasn’t really said anything about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you need to be active or moving when you're not working?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, outside of work I don’t really think about whether I’m needing to move. I might get up and do stuff around the house, but I couldn’t say if that’s because I need to be active or just because there’s always something to do.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had problems at work or in your relationships because of your difficulties with attention or impulsivity?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has caused problems. And at home, my wife gets worn down having to remind me about things I told her I’d handle. It affects daily life pretty clearly.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any of these attention or impulsivity issues when you were in high school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it went back before high school, actually. I’ll cut people off because I think I know where they’re going with the question, and I answer too quick. Then sometimes I realize I missed the important part they hadn’t even said yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others or acting on impulse in non-work situations, like in conversations with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Even outside work, I’ll step into a conversation or jump in while someone’s in the middle of sorting something out, like paperwork or whatever, and I’ll throw my point in before anyone asks. I don’t mean it badly, but it definitely rubs people the wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and activities, or completing them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. My truck’s a mess, my desk is a mess, and even the notes in my phone are all over the place. When I’ve got RFIs, inspections, budget stuff, and crew issues all coming at me, I have a hard time figuring out what to do first. I end up jumping from one thing to another, and then stuff runs late.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're physically restless, like you have excess energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’m on my feet a lot for work anyway, so it’s hard to tell what’s normal. I don’t know that I’d call it excess energy exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with attention and impulsivity, or is this something that started more recently?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on since I was a kid. I remember back in elementary school, teachers were already telling my parents I couldn’t stay in my seat, I’d talk out of turn, lose homework, and rush through directions before I really understood what I was supposed to do. So no, it’s not really new.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have trouble engaging in quiet leisure activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t really do a lot of quiet leisure stuff, so it’s hard for me to say. If I’m sitting around, I usually end up finding something to do, but I don’t know if that’s restlessness or just habit.\n\nDoctor: Do you have problems with anxiety or depression?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say that’s been the main issue for me. I get stressed like anyone else, but I haven’t really thought of it as anxiety or depression.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're always on the go, or like you can't slow down?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I do keep busy most of the time, especially with work, but I don’t know if I’d say I “can’t slow down.” I guess I haven’t paid close attention to it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not at work, like when you're watching TV or sitting with family?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, I haven’t really paid attention to that. If I’m watching TV I might shift around or get up, but I’m not sure if it’s more than normal. My family hasn’t really said anything about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you need to be active or moving when you're not working?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, outside of work I don’t really think about whether I’m needing to move. I might get up and do stuff around the house, but I couldn’t say if that’s because I need to be active or just because there’s always something to do.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had problems at work or in your relationships because of your difficulties with attention or impulsivity?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has caused problems. At work, I’ve missed follow-ups or didn’t keep site coordination organized enough, and it got to the point my employer brought it up formally. At home, my wife gets pretty worn out having to remind me about things I said I’d take care of. It’s frustrating, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you have more energy than your peers?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t usually compare my energy to other people’s. I might keep busy, but I couldn’t say I have more energy than most people.\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with attention and impulsivity caused significant problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s caused real problems. At work, I’ve missed follow-ups and let site coordination get disorganized enough that my employer formally brought it up. And at home, my wife gets worn down having to remind me about things I told her I’d handle. It affects daily life pretty clearly.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any of these attention or impulsivity issues when you were in high school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it went back before high school, actually. In elementary school teachers were already telling my parents I couldn’t stay in my seat, I’d talk out of turn, lose homework, and rush through directions before I really understood what I was supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with attention and impulsivity caused you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. It’s stressful and honestly embarrassing. At work, it got to the point where missed follow-ups and messy site coordination led my employer to formally bring it up. And at home, my wife gets worn down having to remind me about things I already said I’d take care of. So yeah, it bothers me a lot.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Like I said, at work it’s gotten serious enough that my missed follow-ups and disorganized site coordination became a formal concern with my employer. And at home, it puts strain on my marriage because my wife is tired of having to keep reminding me about things I already told her I’d handle.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been diagnosed with attention-deficit/hyperactivity disorder or does your family have a history of it?\n\nPatient: I haven’t ever been formally diagnosed with it, no. I mean, I do move around a lot because of the job, but as far as feeling like I can’t sit still… I haven’t really paid attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others or blurt out answers before they finish speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I’ll cut people off because I think I know where they’re going with the question, and I answer too quick. Then sometimes I realize I missed the important part they hadn’t even said yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others or acting on impulse in non-work situations, like in conversations with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Even outside work, I’ll step into a conversation or jump in while someone’s in the middle of sorting something out, like paperwork or whatever, and I’ll throw my point in before anyone asks. My truck’s a mess, my desk is a mess, and even the notes in my phone are all over the place. So no, it’s not really new.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have trouble engaging in quiet leisure activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t really do a lot of quiet leisure stuff, so it’s hard for me to say. If I’m sitting around, I usually end up finding something to do, but I don’t know if that’s restlessness or just habit.\n\nDoctor: Do you have problems with anxiety or depression?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say that’s been the main issue for me. At home, my wife gets pretty worn out having to remind me about things I said I’d take care of. I might keep busy, but I couldn’t say I have more energy than most people.\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with attention and impulsivity caused significant problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s caused real problems. At work, I’ve missed follow-ups and let site coordination get disorganized enough that my employer formally brought it up. And at home, my wife gets worn down having to remind me about things I told her I’d handle.
